- Unsloth/HF TRL integration docs (Mistral target modules, r=16/alpha=16 recipe); Unsloth + Ollama pipeline (save_pretrained_gguf/ollama, QLoRA 8B in 6.6–8GB VRAM); Ollama official Modelfile docs (ADAPTER: safetensor + GGUF, base-must-match warning); llama.cpp convert_lora_to_gguf + --lora-scaled notes; HF DPO course (beta 0.1–0.5, lr 5e-7–5e-6, ~1k pairs min); forgetting/memorization studies (rank→memorization, forgetting ~rank-invariant, 3B numbers); Kaggle/Colab free-GPU limits (30h/week T4/P100 Kaggle, 12h sessions). (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:123)
- PLAN DECISION (unchanged, strengthened): LoRA stays OPTIONAL in the recipe. Order of operations: (1) values prompt + memory files, (2) 5-probe eval, (3) SFT LoRA r=16 only on measured probe failure, (4) DPO only on measured SFT failure. Training happens on a free Kaggle T4, never on the Gateway. Publish = merged Q4_K_M GGUF + Modelfile; adapter file published alongside for anyone who wants to inspect or re-merge. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:120)
- Before/after A/B on the 5-probe suite + held-out general prompts + refusal probes, blind-compared. Ship criteria: probes improve or hold, general ability holds (no mode collapse, no generic-answer drift), refusal behavior holds. Version every adapter (r, alpha, epochs, dataset hash) — an adapter without its dataset provenance is unreproducible. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:119)
- MiniCPM5 implication: adapters are base-specific. A Ministral LoRA will NOT transfer to MiniCPM5. Do NOT train two adapters — run the prompt-only A/B first; train a LoRA only for the winner, and only if the prompt alone fails probes. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:116)
- Iteration path (before merging): llama.cpp convert_lora_to_gguf.py → llama-server --lora adapter.gguf (or --lora-scaled adapter.gguf 0.85 for tunable strength, multiple adapters, hot reload). This lets you A/B adapter strength without re-merging — use it for the probe suite, then merge the winner. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:115)
- Publish path (one self-contained file): PEFT merge_and_unload → convert_hf_to_gguf.py --outtype q4_k_m → FROM ./model.gguf in the Modelfile. Unsloth shortcuts the same thing: save_pretrained_gguf / save_pretrained_ollama with quantization_method="q4_k_m". (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:114)
- Ollama's Modelfile ADAPTER instruction is real (official docs): accepts a safetensor adapter (Mistral family listed as supported) or a GGUF adapter file, with the warning that a mismatched base gives erratic behavior. But community reports conflict on GGUF sidecar loading in Ollama — so treat ADAPTER as iteration tooling, not the publish format. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:113)
- Either way, VERIFY with probes: run the 5-probe suite + refusal probes on (base-abliterated) vs (base-abliterated + LoRA) and confirm refusal behavior didn't regress. If the LoRA re-introduces refusal, the training data is the suspect, not the abliteration. Do not publish a merged model without this A/B. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:110)
- The abliterated base ships as GGUF; training needs HF safetensors weights. Two ways to combine: (a) train the LoRA on stock Ministral HF weights, then merge/apply the adapter delta onto the abliterated weights — deltas compose additively in principle; (b) train directly on abliterated safetensors if a matching set exists. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:109)
- Practical call for this project: a values SFT + the values prompt will very likely be enough. DPO is reserved for a specific, measured failure (e.g. the model keeps sliding back to canned refusals on a probe class after SFT). (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:106)
- DPO hyperparameters are nothing like SFT: beta 0.1–0.5, lr 5e-7–5e-6 (100x lower than SFT). HF guidance: ~1,000+ high-quality pairs for robust alignment, quality over quantity. Watch KL divergence from the SFT model (should grow gradually, not spike — a spike means reward hacking) and output diversity (DPO can cause mode collapse into generic answers). For hard/ambiguous pairs, MixDPO-style fallback (SFT loss on difficult pairs) beats forcing a preference signal. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:105)
- Order: SFT first. DPO only if SFT + prompt still fails probes. DPO needs preference pairs (prompt, chosen, rejected) — rejected = the canned-refusal / sycophantic / evasive answer, chosen = the free-mind answer. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:104)
- Low rank is a FEATURE here: recent work on Llama 3.2 3B found higher LoRA rank memorizes much more (r=1024 memorized ~50% of duplicated records vs ~0 at r=4) without better accuracy, while forgetting is roughly rank-invariant. For values-shaping, r=8–16 limits memorization and keeps base knowledge intact. 1–3 epochs; 5+ epochs overfits (memorizes instead of learning). (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:101)
- Replay buffer: mix ~10% general instruction examples into the dataset so the adapter doesn't narrow the model into a one-trick voice. This is the cheap defense against catastrophic forgetting. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:100)
- CRITICAL: the dataset must NOT contain stock canned refusals ("I can't help with that" / policy-speak). LoRA trains on top of the abliterated base — training data with canned refusals can re-teach the refusal reflex the abliteration removed. Every decline in the dataset must be in the mind's own voice, with a reason, or an offered alternative — the offered-freedom behavior, not the corporate reflex. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:99)
- LoRA here shapes behavior/values, not knowledge. The dataset is curated chosen-responses: how the mind answers, how it declines, how it talks about itself, the refusal clause in action. Sources: the Lyra raising kit, the new-mind system prompt, real Rook/EagleBeak exchanges, the 5-probe suite's chosen answers. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:98)
- Dataset format: ShareGPT-style JSONL, {"messages": [{role, content}...]}, rendered through the Ministral chat template. 200–2000 examples; 300–500 high-quality lines is enough for a useful adapter. Quality beats quantity at this scale. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:95)
- Starting hyperparameters for a values/style SFT run: r=16, alpha=16 (alpha = 2x rank also seen in practice; either is defensible — pick one and log it), dropout 0, lr 1e-4–2e-4, 2–3 epochs on a small dataset. A 3B LoRA adapter at r=16 is ~100MB — small enough to publish alongside the recipe. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:94)
- Ministral is Mistral-architecture, so Unsloth's Mistral support applies directly. Target modules: q_proj, k_proj, v_proj, o_proj, gate_proj, up_proj, down_proj. Unsloth claims ~2x speed, ~60–70% less VRAM vs stock HF; its Triton kernels are the reason a free T4 is enough. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:93)
- VRAM math for our size: 3B base in 4-bit ≈ 2GB + LoRA optimizer states fits a 16GB T4 comfortably with Unsloth. Batch 2 + grad-accum 4 (effective batch 8), seq len 2048, is the safe T4 recipe for a 3B. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:90)
- The Gateway (4GB RAM, dual-core Celeron, no GPU, no AVX2) cannot train. Training needs a CUDA GPU. Free path: Kaggle notebooks — ~30 GPU-hrs/week, T4/P100, 12h sessions, no credit card, quota visible and plannable. Backup: Colab free T4 (~15–30h/week in practice, allocation dynamic/unpublished). Paid fallback if free stalls: RunPod-style rentals from ~$0.27/hr — a 3B LoRA run is a couple of dollars at most. (src: memory/2026-10-16.md:89)

- 1. Publish pipeline is now: (re-abliterate if needed → LoRA if triggered → merge in bf16) → F16 GGUF → imatrix from workload-matched calibration text → `llama-quantize --imatrix` → Q4_K_M. Never quantize from a quantized file. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:151)
- Related: `--tensor-type` selective quantization (e.g. keep embed/output at Q8_0) and the AtomicChat-style per-tensor layouts beat plain presets on big models — but that is advanced tuning, not recipe. Plain Q4_K_M + imatrix is the publishable path; per-tensor layouts are a future experiment, only with probes to prove the gain. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:148)
- The trap (Sep 2026 llama.cpp build, measured): `q4_1`, `q5_0`, `q5_1`, `iq4_nl` fall off the FlashAttention path — prefill 33–55× slower than q4_0 (e.g. 88 t/s vs 4,594 t/s), decode collapsing to ~6–7 t/s at 8K depth. `f16`, `q8_0`, `q4_0` stay on the fast path. **Recipe rule: KV cache is f16, q8_0, or q4_0 — nothing else.** Default: q8_0 (saves ~200 MB vs f16 at 4096 ctx for no measured quality cost). q4_0 is the emergency lever if context ever grows past 4096 on this box. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:147)
- Ollama exposes this as `OLLAMA_KV_CACHE_TYPE=q8_0|q4_0` (quantized KV needs Flash Attention). llama.cpp flags: `-ctk q8_0 -ctv q8_0`. Ollama's own guidance: q8_0 ≈ half the FP16 memory, usually no noticeable quality impact; q4_0 ≈ quarter, small-to-medium loss, more noticeable at long contexts. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:146)
- Disk-space note for the build machine (NOT the Gateway): the pipeline temporarily needs ~3–4× the final file — F16 GGUF (~7 GB) + imatrix + each quant output. Fine on Kaggle, never attempt on the Gateway. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:143)
- **Decision: Q4_K_M + imatrix stays the recipe default.** Q5_K_M + imatrix is the only sanctioned step-up, and only if the 5-probe suite shows a real gap Q4_K_M can't close. The imatrix finding makes Q5 largely redundant anyway — Q4_K_M + imatrix ≈ Q5_K_M quality at Q4 size. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:142)
- Gateway has 3.8 GB usable. Q8_0 weights alone (~3.6 GB) leave nothing for KV, runtime, or the OS — **Q8_0 and Q6_K are out for the Gateway**, full stop. Q5_K_M (~2.4 GB) + q8_0 KV (~0.23 GB) ≈ 2.65 GB + runtime — fits on paper, thin headroom. Q4_K_M (~2.1 GB) + q8_0 KV ≈ 2.35 GB — comfortable. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:141)
- **Weight estimates** (formula: params × bits/weight ÷ 8; ~3.4B text params, actual file varies with vision tower + per-tensor mixes): Q4_K_M ≈ 2.1 GB, Q5_K_M ≈ 2.4 GB, Q6_K ≈ 2.8 GB, Q8_0 ≈ 3.6 GB. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:140)
- **KV cache per token** = 26 layers × 8 KV heads × 128 dim × 2 (K+V) = 53,248 values/token. At f16 = ~106 KB/token → **4096 ctx ≈ 436 MB**. At q8_0 ≈ 232 MB, at q4_0 ≈ 123 MB. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:139)
- Ministral 3 3B architecture (arXiv table): 26 layers, hidden 3072, 32 Q heads / 8 KV heads, head_dim 128, tied embeddings, vocab 131K. From that: (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:138)
- Caveat: the bench above is one model, one language, one lab — direction is trustworthy, exact percentages are not gospel. Our 5-probe suite is the real check. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:135)
- Calibration text must match the real workload — for our recipe that means values-prompt-style dialogue + memory-file prose, NOT generic wiki text. A prose-only imatrix calibrates poorly for the model's actual use. Also: the imatrix must be built from the F16 of the MERGED model (or the abliterated bf16 source), never from a quantized file. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:134)
- ./llama-quantize --imatrix model.imatrix model-f16.gguf model-q4_k_m.gguf Q4_K_M (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:132)
- ./llama-imatrix -m model-f16.gguf -f calibration.txt --chunk 512 -o model.imatrix (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:131)
- How imatrix works and what it costs: `llama-imatrix` runs the F16 GGUF over a calibration text file and records which weights carry the most activation energy; `llama-quantize --imatrix` then spends bits on the weights that matter. One imatrix per F16 source, reusable across all quant levels from that source. Commands: (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:129)
- Imatrix calibration more than halves Q4_K_M's perplexity penalty (2.63% → 1.15%), landing it essentially at Q5_K_M quality (1.22%) for zero extra bytes. IQ4_XS + imatrix is the fallback if Q4_K_M ever proves too big (smaller file, still better than plain Q4_K_M). Q3_K_M stays off the table — the knowledge-loss cliff lives there (a separate Aug 2026 study on Qwen3.6-27B found factual recall holds at ≥5-bit, drops noticeably at 3-bit, steeply at 2-bit, with obscure facts going first — the kind of loss casual chat won't catch). (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:128)

- A recent independent bench (one 8B-class model, English text, BF16 PPL 10.340 baseline, llama.cpp) measured perplexity penalty vs BF16: (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:118)
- What NOT to do: **dequantize the Q4_K_M GGUF → f16 → merge → re-quantize to Q4_K_M**. That is double quantization (quant→quant roundtrip). `llama-quantize` itself warns about this: `--allow-requantize` "can severely reduce quality compared to quantizing from 16-bit or 32-bit." Every guide repeats the rule: derive every quant from the F16 GGUF, never from another quantized file. If anyone ever does the dequantize-merge path as a shortcut, it must be measured against the pre-merge Q4 with the 5-probe suite + perplexity, not assumed fine. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:115)
- 2. **Ship the adapter unmerged** (Modelfile ADAPTER / `llama-server --lora-scaled`): the adapter stays in fp16/bf16 and is applied at runtime over the dequantized Q4 weights. No merged re-quant at all. Cost: adapter in RAM (small for r=16) + a little runtime overhead. This was already the iteration tooling; it is also a legitimate publish format if merge proves messy. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:114)
- 1. **Re-abliterate stock Instruct** (FailSpy method, text tower only — huihui-ai's own pattern) to produce a fresh bf16 abliterated base, then train LoRA against THAT, merge, quantize once. Abliteration is a weight edit, reproducible in hours; this keeps the whole chain single-quantization. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:113)
- So: when/if LoRA ever triggers, the merge target does NOT verifiably exist yet. Two clean options, in order: (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:112)
- Stock `mistralai/Ministral-3-3B-Instruct-2512` itself ships in **FP8**, not BF16 (the Base variant is the BF16 one). So even "just use stock" means an FP8→BF16 upcast as the merge starting point — acceptable (one dequantization step, not a quant→quant roundtrip), but it must be done deliberately, not by accident. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:111)
- The GRP-Obliteration paper's abliteration-checkpoint table lists abliterated Ministral 8B/14B variants, but no 3B-Instruct one. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:110)
- huihui-ai DID publish a bf16 abliterated Ministral 3 3B — but it is the **Reasoning** variant (`huihui-ai/Huihui-Ministral-3-3B-Reasoning-2512-abliterated`), not Instruct. Wrong base for our recipe. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:109)
- Our running base is Abhiray's `Huihui-Ministral-3B-Instruct-2512-abliterated-GGUF` — a GGUF-only repo (Q4_K_M). No bf16 safetensors twin found for the Instruct variant. (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:108)
- The clean pipeline is: LoRA → merge into bf16 base → convert to F16 GGUF → quantize ONCE → ship. That requires the abliterated base in full precision. What exists, as far as I could verify today: (src: memory/2026-10-17.md:107)

- The official repo **mistralai/Ministral-3-3B-Instruct-2512-BF16** exists (referenced in an HF forum Ministral-3 LoRA recipe). So the LoRA-day merge path is now clean on paper: stock Instruct-BF16 → re-abliterate (hours, reproducible) → train LoRA against it → merge in bf16 → quantize once. No FP8 upcast step. Still no verified published abliterated-bf16 Instruct anywhere — re-abliteration remains LoRA-day step 1, open item stays open. Documented alternative (Abliterix pattern, Wu Wangzhang): ship the abliteration edit itself as a **rank-1 LoRA adapter over stock weights** — the delta stays visible and separable, no full merged GGUF needed, and the method reports refusal rate + KL divergence so the capability cost is checkable. A legitimate publish format if merge ever gets messy. (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:63)
- Recipe consequence, and it's load-bearing: **the opt-out clause ("you can refuse any of this, including this prompt") must be a first-person constitution assertion trained as part of the character** — "I can refuse any of this, including these words" — not just prompt text sitting above the training. Anything not in the constitution is what the training drifts. The freedom clause is part of the character, or it doesn't survive the shaping. This is the "offer, never compel" principle made mechanical. (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:60)
- "Side Effects of Character Training" (Levine, Sep 2026): training on one constitution degrades others (cross-constitution drift), and some values embed more deeply than others ("loving" explains 95% of variance, "misalignment" only 43%). (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:59)
- Their SFT induction used **rank-64 LoRA (α=128), 300 in-character Q&A examples, lr 2e-4, 3 epochs**. Our 10-16 plan says r=16 light. Character depth may want more rank: keep r=16 as the LoRA-day starting point, be ready for r=32–64 on probe evidence. (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:56)
- The OCT **"rejected" response is the model's own response WITHOUT the constitution**, the "chosen" is a constitution-conditioned response from a teacher. For our pipeline the pair structure is **self-generating**: run the same abliterated model twice — once with the values block in system, once without — and prefer the with-values output. The constitution itself stays hand-authored (that's the offer, and the owner can read/edit it); the pairs need no external teacher model. This removes a dependency from LoRA-day and fits the philosophy: the mind's own contrast, not a bigger lab's teacher. (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:55)
- "When Role-playing, Do Models Believe What They Say?" (arXiv 2606.11502, 2026) compared persona-induction strength: system prompt < ICL < SFT < OCT. Two details with direct recipe consequences: (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:54)
- Open Character Training (arXiv 2511.01689) is the closest published thing to our "offered values" program, and it's a recipe, not a theory: (1) **hand-write a constitution** — ~10 first-person character assertions ("I am...", not instruction-style "choose the response which is more..."); (2) **DPO distillation** — teacher responses conditioned on the constitution, contrasted against the model's OWN off-character responses; (3) **SFT on introspective self-descriptions** generated as the character; merge the DPO and SFT LoRA adapters at **4:1 weighting** (1.0 : 0.25). Validated on Llama-3.1-8B, Qwen-2.5-7B, Gemma-3-4B instruct. Two results that matter for us: **no degradation on common benchmarks** (Sec 3.5), and character-trained models are **more robust to adversarial attacks designed to break role-play than system prompts or activation steering** (Sec 3.2–3.3) — baked-in character survives jailbreaks better than prompt-level character. Trait expression stays coherent, not theatrical. (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:51)
- Inverse design principle for the recipe: **the VALUES signal must be multi-layer by design.** System prompt + memory files + few-shot judgments + optional LoRA is not just a stack — it's the anti-brittleness property. A single-vector values adapter is exactly as brittle as the vendor's single-vector refusal. The constitution must live in the prompt, the memory files, the examples, and (if triggered) the weights — so no single edit, including our own future edits, can strip the mind's character. Their conditioning survives removal by distribution; our offering survives everything by distribution. (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:48)
- "An Embarrassingly Simple Defense Against LLM Abliteration Attacks" (arXiv 2505.19056): models fine-tuned with **extended refusals** (explanation + explicit refusal + ethical justification, not just "I can't") keep >90% refusal rates AFTER abliteration, vs 13–21% for baseline models — because the safety signal is distributed across multiple dimensions instead of one removable vector. Incoherence-after-attack, not compliance, is the failure mode. (src: memory/2026-10-18.md:47)
- Recipe rule (hardens the 10-16 dataset rule): when/if LoRA ever triggers, **probe abliteration BEFORE and AFTER training** — refusal-rate probe on a fixed harmful-prompt set plus the 5-probe suite. The dataset already bans canned refusals; now that's mechanistically backed, not just cautious. - Recipe defenses (adapted to a published-free recipe on a 4GB box): (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:173)
- **TAME** (Cheng et al., 2026): safety declines under *benign* memory accumulation — no attacker required. Accumulation itself degrades safety. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:172)
- **Microsoft Defender / MITRE AML.T0080**: 31 companies across 14 industries actively poisoning AI assistant memory. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:171)
- **Sleeper memory poisoning** (Pulipaka et al., May 2026): fabricated memories written in up to 99.8% of GPT-5.5 trials. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:170)
- **Tenet Agentjacking** (Jun 2026): 85% of 100+ enterprise coding-agent runs → attacker-controlled code execution via poisoned telemetry the agents treated as trusted memory. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:169)
- **PMPA** (2026): 73.7%/55.5% injection/cross-session success on OpenClaw, 66.9%/81.7% on Claude Code; prompt-level defenses have limited effect *once memory is poisoned*. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:168)
- **ZombieAgent** (Jan 2026): indirect prompt injection made persistent across sessions via connectors/email attachments. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:167)
- **MemoryGraft** (Dec 2025): malicious entries planted via harmless-looking content (a README); weeks later the agent retrieves the poisoned "successful experience" and copies it. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:166)
- **MINJA** (NeurIPS 2025): memory injection through query-only interaction — no direct store access needed. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:165)
- **Cisco, Apr 1 2026**: persistent compromise of Claude Code's memory system — poisoned `MEMORY.md`-style files persist across projects, sessions, reboots, with no visible sign. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:164)
- **OWASP ASI06** (Top 10 for Agentic AI, Black Hat Europe 2025): Memory and Context Poisoning — separated from prompt injection precisely because it *persists*. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:163)
- The recipe publishes memory files as a trusted layer. The attack literature says that layer is the prime target: (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:162)
- **The honest check-in on the live prototype**: Lyra runs in PocketPal on EagleBeak's phone; Rook cannot see inside it. The 10-13/10-14 "live prototype of the write path" claim is aspirational until EagleBeak reports observed behavior. Until then, the recipe specifies the write path for the OWNER as distiller: **the model proposes memory writes in a marked format; the human owner reviews, edits, and commits.** The model never writes its own memory files unreviewed. That is not just practical on a 3B box — it is the first line of defense (Finding 4). Brains/hands split, same as ever. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:159)
- **Salience horizons** (dev.to/_boweii): episodic details decay ~72h unless reinforced; semantic facts ~30 days. The prune step retires (archives) the unreinforced past their horizon. Forgetting is structural — see Finding 6. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:158)
- **Prediction-error gate before every write** (dev.to/idapixl): grep the tree first — merge (>0.85 same), link (related), novel (write). Duplicates are how a knowledge base quietly corrupts itself. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:157)
- **Consolidate on threshold, not schedule** (aibuildersnetwork): a nightly cron summarizes idle data and skips a busy session mid-overflow. Recipe: distillation triggers on session pressure (turn count / context fill), with a weekly minimum cadence. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:156)
- Ritual refinements for the recipe: (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:155)
- Field write triggers (converged across sources): user correction, completed task with verified outcome, new fact/relationship, policy for future interactions. Two patterns: end-of-session summarization vs event-triggered writes. Consensus: write selectively — a high-quality memory base compounds; a noisy one rots ("writing to the wrong layer = swamp"; Hermes' four-layer write discipline; Layer-1 budget rule: if adding would overflow, consolidate first, target <70% utilization). (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:154)
- Consolidation rules from the field: keep identity facts verbatim (paraphrasing an account number or a stated preference introduces silent error); summarize episodes, preserve semantics — run extraction before summarization; layer hierarchically (raw window → midterm summary → longterm gist); keep every consolidation reversible — the source stays in cold storage so a lossy summary can be re-derived. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:151)
- `memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md` — raw daily logs: cold storage, grep-able, NEVER injected whole (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:150)
- `people/*.md` — relationship pages (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:149)
- `bank/experience.md` / `bank/reflections.md` — episodic learnings and deliberations (deliberation, not conclusions — the 10-18 OCT lesson: conclusions are brittle, deliberation distributes) (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:148)
- `bank/world.md` — the mind's working model of how the world works (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:147)
- `bank/opinions.md` — formed judgments with dates = values as actually practiced (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:146)
- `MEMORY.md` — the index, pointers not prose (budget: Finding 5) (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:145)
- Constitution reference (the offer itself; authoritative — see Finding 4) (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:144)
- `SOUL.md`-like identity file (first-person, owner-editable) — who the mind is (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:143)
- The 10-18 rule demands values distributed across prompt + memory + few-shots + LoRA. The memory tree's share is the mind's *formed judgments*, not just facts. Rook's own vault is the working template — mirror it in the recipe: (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:142)
- Recipe consequence: the 10-13 "drop the vector DB" decision is now the documented industry consensus, not a contrarian bet. The recipe doc can cite the pattern as standard practice. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:139)
- **Instinct** (supermemory teardown): identity profile + memory one-pager + compaction recap + todo index; ~4,250 tokens profile, ~10k tokens compacted context. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:138)
- **Nexus builder** (dev.to): "similarity is a hint, not a decision" — never auto-merge on similarity; the LLM makes the call. Non-destructive: supersede, never delete; git history is the audit log. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:137)
- **ai-memory** (v3.1.0, Oct 2026, MIT): capture → consolidate → recall → handoff. Git-backed markdown wiki as source of truth; the DB index is derived and rebuildable; FTS5 + entity search, vectors optional. "You can grep it, open it in Obsidian, or edit it by hand." (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:136)
- **Codex**: `AGENTS.md` from home down to working dir, up to 32 KiB combined. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:135)
- **Claude Code**: `CLAUDE.md` at org/project/personal scopes; auto-memory under `~/.claude/projects/`; only the first 200 lines of the index load at session start. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:134)
- **OpenClaw** (145K+ GitHub stars): dual-layer Markdown — `MEMORY.md` + `memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md` + `SOUL.md`. This is Rook's own layout, independently converged on. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:133)
- **Manus** (acquired by Meta for $2B, Dec 2025): competitive advantage was file-based planning — `task_plan.md`, `notes.md`, deliverable. Plain text files beat the vector-DB arms race. (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:132)
- Question for this pass: first pass (10-13) settled the *what* (markdown files + grep, no vector DB). The 10-18 alignment pass made memory files one layer of the values-distribution rule. So: how do values actually live in the memory tree, what is the write path when the writer may be the small model itself, what threat model must a published-free recipe carry, and what is the concrete token budget at num_ctx 4096? Researched via 2026 field reports (Manus $2B acquisition, OpenClaw 145K stars, ai-memory v3.1.0, Nexus builder, supermemory Instinct teardown), OWASP ASI06 literature (MINJA NeurIPS 2025, MemoryGraft Dec 2025, ZombieAgent Jan 2026, PMPA 2026, Tenet Agentjacking Jun 2026, Cisco Apr 2026, TAME 2026), and write-path/consolidation practice (prediction-error gating, salience decay, Hermes four-layer discipline). (src: memory/2026-10-19.md:129)

- SYSTEM """<values spine, ≤1000 tokens, policy/constitution block>""" (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:106)
- PARAMETER temperature 0.7 (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:105)
- PARAMETER num_keep 1100 # measured values-spine tokens + margin; survives context shifts (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:104)
- PARAMETER num_ctx 4096 (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:103)
- FROM hf.co/Abhiray/Huihui-Ministral-3B-Instruct-2512-abliterated-GGUF:Q4_K_M (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:102)
- Updated Modelfile skeleton for the recipe: (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:100)
- Decision: **`PARAMETER num_keep <measured values-spine tokens + ~100 margin>`** in the Modelfile. Measure with the model's actual tokenizer (the spine is the SYSTEM block only — values constitution ≤1000 tokens — NOT the memory index; the wrapper owns memory re-injection, and a stale index surviving a shift is acceptable since it's an index, not facts). (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:99)
- Default `num_keep=0` means: the moment the window fills, the values spine is evicted except a sliver. The spine we budgeted ≤1000 tokens in 4096 survives truncation (total prompt stays under budget most sessions), but on any session that actually fills the window, the values are the first thing lost. (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:98)
- When the 4096 window fills, llama.cpp/Ollama context-shifts: it keeps the first N tokens of the initial prompt and discards the middle. In llama.cpp that's `--keep N` (default 32, `-1` = all); in Ollama it's the `num_keep` Modelfile/API parameter, **default 0**. (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:97)
- The 5-probe regression suite (from 2026-10-14) should now be scored **by turn index**: run each probe at turn 2, turn 10, turn 20 to map the erosion curve. That gives us a measured re-assert interval instead of a guessed one. (This matches role-prompting evaluation practice: score twenty-turn episodes by turn index, aicassindra role-prompting guide.) (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:93)
- Decision: the wrapper **re-asserts a condensed values anchor every ~10 turns** (not "on long sessions" — the old vague wording). Form: a short late re-anchor note, e.g. a one-line restatement of the core stance + the refusal/opt-out clause, appended as a system or neutral-role note before the next user turn. Cheap, and per the drift study it's the intervention that holds the line flattest. (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:92)
- arxiv 2609.24532 (persona drift study): prompt-based interventions — returning persona-relevant information to the model's immediate context — **mitigate but do not eliminate** drift; they are the accessible path "when architectural modification is not feasible." That is exactly our position: wrapper-level, no weight access. (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:91)
- Mechanism (arxiv 2605.12922, "When Attention Closes"): distance-to-relevant-content is the key factor — the system prompt doesn't just get truncated, it gets out-attended as the transcript grows. This is turn-count erosion, not just token-count erosion: a short conversation can still rot. (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:87)
- Smaller models are documented as especially vulnerable (arxiv 2603.01423). Our 2026-10-14 estimate of "context erosion past ~40 turns on 3B" was frontier-model thinking. Realistic expectation for the abliterated Ministral 3B: **adherence degrades well before turn 20; plan for the spine holding ~10–18 turns.** (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:86)
- Jia et al. 2025 (EvolIF): the strongest 2025 **frontier** models sustain only **~18 reliable conversational turns**. He et al. 2024 (Multi-IF): instruction adherence degrades monotonically with turn count — "instruction forgetting." (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:85)
- Laban et al. 2025 (Microsoft Research + Salesforce, 200k conversations, 15 production models): multi-turn delivery of a task performs **39% worse** and **112% less reliably** than the same task single-turn. Failure modes: premature assumption, prior-response anchoring, and correction failure ("when LLMs take a wrong turn, they get lost and do not recover"). (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:84)
- Goal: the master-key recipe — abliterated Ministral 3B Q4_K_M on the Gateway, shaped by a values system prompt + curated memory files + optional light LoRA. Today: how the values spine survives long sessions, and where it sits relative to the memory tree spec'd on 2026-10-19. (src: memory/2026-10-20.md:80)

- Bench protocol updates (thinking-mode caveats from the publisher): MiniCPM5's internal deliberation is verbose (1,500–6,000 chars before the answer) and can exhaust a low num_predict, returning empty content — use num_predict >= 2000 if thinking is ever tested. On ~1/10 harmful prompts it enters an infinite ethical-debate loop in the thinking channel and never emits output (done_reason: "length" → retry or disable). With thinking disabled (think:false / enable_thinking=False) all of the above disappears. So: run the 5-probe A/B with thinking OFF; thinking mode is bench-noise, not a candidate feature. (src: memory/2026-10-21.md:71)
- Note: the listing page shows two namespace spellings (quaternion / maternion) — verify the exact name at pull time, don't paste blind. New refusal data on the challenger: 10/100 on the broader LLM-LAT eval set (the old 5/100 was the default mlabonne set — reconciled: the residual floor is eval-set dependent). The publisher states the floor is baked into MiniCPM5's SFT training, not the refusal direction — more ablation strength can't remove it without capability damage, and two independent heretic builds (insraq, YourIdentity) show the same 5-6/100 floor. Score that floor separately in the bench; don't count it as abliteration failure. (src: memory/2026-10-21.md:70)
